21|
22|Source: /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261018-96h/olson/continuations/terminal-pplus1-kp2-exclusion/proof-KP2-EXCL-v1.md, lines 226-229.
23|
24|Old:
25|````text
26|  (F1) Single-run omission counts in the style of PP1-EXCL (Lemma SR, K = p-1 omitted letters, n = 2p-4): with b letters != a,
27|       a run with A - alpha_i >= p-1 has b + alpha_i - beta_i nonzero weights, so OM' forces alpha_i <= beta_i + (p-2-b). For
28|       b = p-2 this gives alpha_i <= beta_i and two empty runs, but for b = p-3 (alpha_i <= beta_i + 1) and b = p-4
29|       (alpha_i <= beta_i + 2) single runs alone do not force empty runs; the set window (W) with Step 4 is what closes these.
30|````
31|
32|New:
33|````text
34|  (F1) Re-derived single-run omission count, using no pending SR/OM' interface. Take z=-1 on run i, omit exactly p-1 letters
35|       to select n=2p-4, and assume A-alpha_i>=p-1 signed-a letters outside that run. Among the other letters the number of
36|       nonzero deviations from signed value a is b+alpha_i-beta_i. If this is >=p-1, choose p-1 such occurrences; their
37|       nonzero deviations have all of Z_p as subset sums by RS(b). Supplement any chosen subset with outside signed-a
38|       occurrences to omit exactly p-1 letters. The complementary selections attain every signed sum, including T, so SEL2
39|       contradicts avoidance. Thus b+alpha_i-beta_i<=p-2. If b=p-2 then A=2p-3 and at most one run fails the filler hypothesis
40|       (two would require 2p-2>A a-letters); each nonempty remaining run consumes a non-a letter, giving at least two empty
41|       runs. For b=p-3 or p-4 this same counting argument allows alpha_i<=beta_i+1 or +2 and does not itself give that
42|       empty-run bound; the set window and light-set argument in section 4 supply it.
43|````

59|## E-S2-1: isolate Sublemma (a) at its exact elementary hypotheses (Claude S2-1; source lines 36-40 and 101-102)
60|Old (B2-1(a)): '(a) A := Sigma(U) u {0} (Sigma: nonempty subset sums), sigma := sigma(U). Prefix sums of the zero-sum-free U
61|(any order) are distinct and nonzero, so |A| >= 2m + 4. Complementation W -> U - W gives A = sigma - A. A avoids [1, 2m - 2],
62|hence also [sigma - 2m + 2, sigma - 1]. As sigma is in Sigma(U), sigma is not in [0, 2m - 2]. If sigma >= 4m - 3, the two arcs
63|are disjoint (sigma - 2m + 2 >= 2m - 1) and lie in [1, p - 1], so |A| <= p - (4m - 4) = 2m + 3: contradiction. So
64|2m - 1 <= sigma <= 4m - 4, the arcs merge into [1, sigma - 1], and A <= {0} u [sigma, p - 1].'
65|New: '(a) Sublemma (a) (the elementary argument here isolated with all its hypotheses). Let p = 6m - 1 be prime, m >= 3.
66|Let V be zero-sum-free over Z_p, |V| = 2m + 3, with no nonempty subset-sum residue in [1, 2m - 2]. Put
67|A_V := {0} u Sigma(V), sigma_V := sigma(V), taking sigma_V in [0, p - 1]. Then
68|  sigma_V in [2m - 1, 4m - 4], and A_V subset {0} u [sigma_V, p - 1].
69|Proof of sublemma. Zero-sum-freeness gives sigma_V != 0 and distinct nonzero prefix sums (any order); hence
70||A_V| >= |V| + 1 = 2m + 4. By hypothesis sigma_V is not in [1, 2m - 2], so sigma_V >= 2m - 1. Complementation, including empty
71|and full occurrence subsets, gives A_V = sigma_V - A_V, so A_V avoids both [1, 2m - 2] and
72|[sigma_V - 2m + 2, sigma_V - 1]. If sigma_V >= 4m - 3 these are disjoint nonwrapping intervals in [1, p - 1], excluding
73|4m - 4 residues and forcing |A_V| <= p - (4m - 4) = 2m + 3, a contradiction. Otherwise their union is [1, sigma_V - 1], proving
74|the claimed containment and the upper bound sigma_V <= 4m - 4. End of sublemma.
75|Apply it here to V = U: delta0 = 1 gives |U| = 2m + 3, U is zero-sum-free by (F*), and (F*) excludes every nonempty subset-sum
76|residue in [0, 2m - 2]. Write A := A_U and sigma := sigma_U. This gives exactly the original conclusion of (a).'

88|## E-B2: triple lower bound obtained from a lifted pair (Claude B-2; source lines 47-48)
89|Old: 'If t >= 4, a proper triple has integer sum in [18m - 1 - 2B, 3B]'.
90|New: 'If t >= 4, a proper triple contains a pair whose integer sum is p + y with y in J, hence at least
91|p + (6m - B) = 12m - 1 - B; adding the third term, which is at least 6m - B, gives the triple lower bound
92|18m - 1 - 2B. Its upper bound is 3B. Thus a proper triple has integer sum in [18m - 1 - 2B, 3B]'.
93|
94|## E-B1: explicit pair/triple wrap windows at B = 4m (Claude B-1; source lines 51-52)
95|Old: '(e) B = 4m, J = [2m, 4m]. Two terms: z + z' in [4m, 8m] with residue in J: z + z' = 4m (z = z' = 2m)
96|or z + z' in [8m - 1, 8m]. A proper triple (t >= 4) has sum in [8m - 1, 10m - 1];'
97|New: '(e) B = 4m, J = [2m, 4m]. For an integer pair/triple sum S of terms in J (so 4m <= S <= 12m = 2p + 2), its residue is
98|S if S < p; 0 if S = p or S = 2p; S - p if p < S < 2p; and 1 or 2 if S = 2p + 1 or 2p + 2. Since 0, 1, 2 are outside J
99|(m >= 3), a residue in J forces S in J before wrapping or S - p in J after one wrap. A pair sum is in [4m, 8m], so before
100|wrapping it must be S = 4m (both terms 2m), and after one wrap it lies in
101|[p + 2m, p + 4m] intersect [4m, 8m] = [8m - 1, 8m]. A proper triple (t >= 4) is in [6m, 12m], so it cannot land in J without
102|wrapping, cannot hit 0 or residues 1, 2 at the second wrap, and must lie in p + J = [8m - 1, 10m - 1];'
103|The subsequent high-pair and surviving-triple argument is unchanged.
104|
105|## E-S2-2: unused size bound (Claude S2-2; source line 86)
106|Old: 'every s in [0, S'] is the x-sum of some W'_s, with |W'_s| >= s - E' (E' := S' - |U'|).'
107|New: 'every s in [0, S'] is the x-sum of some W'_s. The accompanying bound |W'_s| >= s - E' (E' := S' - |U'|) is valid but unused
108|in Sec. 4.'
109|
110|## E-S2-3: negative signed sum (Claude S2-3, optional, adopted; source lines 98-99)
111|Old: 'If -24: s* <= 16 forces sigma~(Z*) <= -8, but the negative part of Z0 has total |sum| <= 1
112|(signed sum 0, at most one +1): contradiction.'
113|New: 'If -24: s* <= 16 forces sigma~(Z*) <= -8. But the signed sum of Z0 is 0, and q <= 1 of its terms have signed representative
114|+1; therefore the negative signed representatives of Z0 sum to -q >= -1. Every signed subset sum sigma~(Z*) is consequently
115|>= -q >= -1, contradicting sigma~(Z*) <= -8.'
116|
117|## Disposition and scope
118|All Claude findings are disposed above; Codex required no repair. Old source/proof/package/excerpt files are preserved unchanged.
119|L1 and B2-1 retain their threshold-counterexample setting (p = 6m - 1 prime, m >= 3, kappa = 4m, g = g0). The two full LS
120|claims retain the exact scope copied in the package:
121|- every odd Y over C_34 with |Y| >= 42 and no zero-sum length in [26, 34] has no zero-sum length >= 36;
122|- every odd Y over C_46 with |Y| >= 56 and no zero-sum length in [34, 46] has no zero-sum length >= 48.
123|No proof of the full BASE2 family S3, LS+, STAB, INV* or ordered Olson follows from this package. The candidate's Sec. 5 remains
124|planning context, not a classification of all unresolved cases. No novelty/priority claim. No further S3 derivation was begun
125|while this erratum was prepared.

40|Let d := |x~| in [1, (p - 1)/2] (signed representative).  The n distinct points a + rho x (1 <= rho <= n) lie in I n (I + x), so
41||I n (I + x)| >= n, and for an arc of l < p consecutive residues |I n (I + x)| = max(0, l - d) + max(0, l - p + d).
42|(i) d >= 2n: then l <= 2n <= d and l - p + d <= 2d - p < 0, so I n (I + x) is EMPTY, contradicting n >= 1 points.
43|(ii) p - 2n < d < 2n: each nonzero term is < n (l - d < 2n - (p - 2n) = 4n - p < n; l - p + d < 4n - p < n) and if both are
44|positive their sum is 2l - p <= 4n - p < n; so |I n (I + x)| < n: contradiction.
45|(iii) Otherwise d <= p - 2n and d < 2n, so l - p + d <= 0 and n <= l - d: d <= l - n <= n.
46|Lifting (induction on rho).  Let L = [alpha, alpha + l - 1] be an integer interval mapping onto I, y_0 in L the lift of a, and
47|y_rho := y_0 + rho x~.  If y_rho is in L, then y_(rho+1) is in [alpha - n, alpha + l - 1 + n] <= [alpha - n, alpha + 3n - 1] and
48|its residue lies in I, so y_(rho+1) is in L + mp for some integer m; m >= 1 is impossible (alpha + p > alpha + 3n - 1 as p > 3n - 1)
49|and m <= -1 is impossible (alpha + l - 1 - p < alpha - n as l + n - 1 <= 3n - 1 < p).  So y_(rho+1) is in L, and by induction
50|y_0, y_n are in L: n d <= l - 1 <= 2n - 1, d = 1, x = +-1.  []

60|-sigma(U') would be a nonempty zero-sum of U', and one containing it has a nonempty complement in U' that is a zero-sum.  Its length
61||U'| + 1 >= p - 3e + 4 >= (p + 3)/2 = floor(p/2) + 2 (p >= 6e - 5), so the index-one theorem (record e4615144..., n = p) gives a != 0 and
62|integers x >= 1, one for each term, with every term equal to x a and the x summing to ord(a) = p; the coefficient of -sigma(U') is
63|>= 1, so S' := sum over U' of x_u <= p - 1 (and delta' + E' <= 3e - 3).  The greedy step of proof-LSplus-e2-v1.md Sec. 3 (C), applied with
64|r := |U'| and S := S' (its hypothesis S' <= p - 1 < p + 1 <= 2|U'| holds, as p >= 6e - 5), gives every integer in [0, S'] as the x-sum of a

18|## Item 1 (Codex M2; Claude m1, m9): Lemma MAX -- integer witness, c = 1, e >= 1
19|1a. Old (A 13-14):
20|    | Proof.  Write y = c h, c odd in [1, 2p - 1].  For c in {1, 3} the sequence is h^a (3h)^b with a + b = 3kappa + 2 and still has
21|    | a long zero-sum, so Lemma TV forces a violation of (ZP_kappa).  Let c >= 5 (indeed c >= 3 suffices below).
22|New:
23|    | Proof.  Every odd y equals c h for a unique odd c in [1, 2p - 1] (h is a generator); this includes the involution y = p
24|    | (c = p).  For c in {1, 3} the sequence is h^a (3h)^b with a + b = 3kappa + 2 and still has a long zero-sum (that of E2,
25|    | resp. E3), so Lemma TV forces a violation of (ZP_kappa).  For c = 1, Lemma TV is load-bearing: the level argument below
26|    | fails there (w1 = p > p - 1 >= j2 for E2; w1 = 2p > 2p - 1 >= j2 for E3).  Let now 3 <= c <= 2p - 1 (c = 3 is covered twice).
27|1b. Old (A 18):
28|    | w2 := (2p + 2e - 1 - c)/2 (integers; w2 - w1 = e - 1 >= 1).  The two intervals meet: w1 <= p - 1 (c >= 3) and
29|New:
30|    | w2 := (2p + 2e - 1 - c)/2 (integers, c being odd; w2 - w1 = e - 1 >= 0, for which e >= 1 suffices).  The two intervals
31|    | meet: w1 <= p - 1 (c >= 3) and
32|1c. Old (A 20):
33|    | (2p + 3e - 2 - c)/3 <= p - 1 (c >= 3e + 1 - p, true as 3e <= p - 1) and <= (4p - c)/3 (3e - 2 <= 2p), so j1 <= j2; w1 <= w2.
34|New:
35|    | j1 <= j2: put x := (2p + 3e - 2 - c)/3 and x' := (4p - c)/3.  Then x' - x = a/3 >= (p + 3)/3 > 1 (a = 2p - 3e + 2 >= p + 3
36|    | as 3e <= p - 1), so ceil(x) < x + 1 <= x' and hence ceil(x) <= floor(x'); x <= p - 1 (c >= 3e + 1 - p, true as
37|    | 3e <= p - 1), so ceil(x) <= p - 1; 0 <= floor(x') as 4p - c >= 2p + 1; and 0 <= p - 1.  So max(0, ceil(x)) <=
38|    | min(p - 1, floor(x')), i.e. j1 <= j2 (in particular 0 <= j2).  w1 <= w2 (e >= 1).  Integer witness: j1 <= j2, w1 <= w2,
39|    | w1 <= j2 and j1 <= w2 are inequalities between integers, so j* := max(j1, w1) <= min(j2, w2); with i := 4p - c - 3j* in
40|    | [0, a] and j* in [0, p - 1], y h^i (3h)^(j*) is a zero-sum subsequence of E2 y of length 1 + 4p - c - 2j* in
41|    | [2kappa + 2, 2p].
42|1d. Old (A 24):
43|    | (5p + 3e - 2 - c)/3 <= w2 (c <= 2p + 1); (5p + 3e - 2 - c)/3 <= 2p - 1 (c >= 3e + 1 - p) and <= (6p - c)/3 (3e - 2 <= p).
44|New:
45|    | j1 <= w2: (5p + 3e - 2 - c)/3 <= w2 (c <= 2p + 1) and w2 >= p + e > 0 (c <= 2p - 1).  j1 <= j2: put
46|    | x := (5p + 3e - 2 - c)/3 and x' := (6p - c)/3; x' - x = a/3 >= 1 (a = 3kappa - 2p + 2 >= 3 as 3kappa >= 2p + 1; the
47|    | margin is exactly 1 when a = 3), so ceil(x) <= floor(x'); x <= 2p - 1 (c >= 3e + 1 - p); 0 <= floor(x') (6p - c >= 4p + 1);
48|    | hence j1 <= j2.  w1 <= w2 (e >= 1).  Integer witness j* := max(j1, w1) <= min(j2, w2): with i := 6p - c - 3j* in [0, a]
49|    | and j* in [0, 2p - 1], y h^i (3h)^(j*) is a zero-sum subsequence of E3 y of length 1 + 6p - c - 2j* in [2kappa + 2, 2p].

51|## Item 2 (Codex M1; Claude m2): Corollary SHIFT-C, sigma(Y) != 0 via a SHORTEST long zero-sum and odd-kappa parity
52|Old (P 63-64):
53|    |   (|X| <= 3kappa - 2p <= 2e + 1, so a zero-sum in X would have length <= 2e and miss T); in particular sigma(Y) != 0 (else X,
54|    |   nonempty by SLZ3, would be a zero-sum).
55|New:
56|    |   (|X| <= 3kappa - 2p <= 2e + 1, so a nonempty zero-sum in X would have even length in [2, 2e] and miss T, against the
57|    |   first bullet; for T = Y, X is empty and the claim is vacuous); in particular sigma(Y) != 0: for kappa odd, |Y| = 3kappa + 2
58|    |   is odd and a sum of an odd number of odd elements is odd; for kappa even (indeed for every kappa), if sigma(Y) = 0, choose
59|    |   T to be a SHORTEST long zero-sum of Y: SLZ3 gives |T| <= 3kappa + 1 < |Y|, so X = Y - T is nonempty with
60|    |   sigma(X) = sigma(Y) - sigma(T) = 0, a nonempty zero-sum in the zero-sum-free X: contradiction.
61|(For an arbitrary long T the complement may be empty -- T = Y when sigma(Y) = 0 -- which is why the shortest T is chosen.)

70|    | 0 <= kappa' <= kappa0, (ZP_kappa') implies (S1_kappa'), so a (ZP_kappa') sequence has no kappa'-long zero-sum.
71|    | Sequences are families of odd elements indexed by occurrences; a subsequence is a set of occurrences; 'Z meets T' means that
72|    | Z and T share an occurrence, 'misses' is its negation, and Y - Z, Y - T, Y - y, Y - z - z' remove occurrences.  Every
73|    | nonempty zero-sum of odd elements has even length.
74|3b. Old (P 59):
75|    | makes it fail (S1_kappa').  Fact S excludes kappa' <= kappa0.  LS+(p, kappa') gives |Y| - z <= 3kappa' + 1.  []

127|Old (A 37-40):
128|    | ## Side observation on SLZ3 (internal; not a claim beyond the SLZ3 text)
129|    | Re-running SLZ3's Steps at N = 3kappa + 1 (kappa odd) gives base = (3kappa + 3 - 2p)/2 >= 3 whenever 3kappa >= 2p + 3 (3kappa + 3 - 2p is even), so
130|    | for kappa >= kappa0 + 2 the bound improves to N <= 3kappa; F1 shows N = 3kappa + 1 occurs at kappa = (2p + 1)/3.  In all
131|    | cases N <= max(3kappa, 2p + 2).
132|New:
133|    | ## Candidate SO (SEPARATE, UNREVIEWED; status HOLD; not granted, used nowhere)
134|    | Candidate SO.  Let p >= 5 be prime and kappa0 + 1 <= kappa <= p - 1.  Under (ZP_kappa), every shortest kappa-long zero-sum T of
135|    | a sequence of odd elements has length N <= max(3kappa, 2p + 2); equivalently N <= 3kappa unless 3kappa = 2p + 1 (then
136|    | p = 1 (mod 3), kappa = kappa0 + 1 and N <= 2p + 2 = 3kappa + 1, attained by F1).  No claim is made for kappa >= p (at
137|    | kappa = p, g^(4p) has shortest long zero-sum of length 4p > 3p + 1; SLZ3 erratum 1, M5), and no sharpness is claimed for
138|    | kappa >= kappa0 + 2.
139|    | Proof sketch and step map (recorded for a later review; not granted).  The accepted SLZ3 gives N <= 3kappa + 1; N is even.
140|    | (i) kappa even (includes kappa = p - 1, and kappa = kappa0 + 1 = (2p + 2)/3 when p = 2 (mod 3)): 3kappa + 1 is odd, so
141|    |     N <= 3kappa.
142|    | (ii) kappa odd and 3kappa = 2p + 1: max(3kappa, 2p + 2) = 3kappa + 1 and SLZ3 is the claim.
143|    | (iii) kappa odd and 3kappa >= 2p + 2: then 3kappa >= 2p + 3 (3kappa is odd), equivalently kappa >= kappa0 + 2 in the range.
144|    |     Suppose N = 3kappa + 1; with u := 2kappa: l := N - u = kappa + 1 (even), D := 2u - N = kappa - 1, delta := 2kappa - p
145|    |     (odd, 3 <= delta <= p - 2).  Re-run SLZ3's proof (S1-sharp Steps 1-9 with S1-sharp erratum 1 and SLZ3 erratum 1 as
146|    |     mandatory overlays) with SLZ3's hypothesis N >= 3kappa + 2 replaced by N = 3kappa + 1:
147|    |     - Step 1 (minimality of N only): proper nonempty zero-sums of T have length in [l, u]; N >= 2p + 4 gives D <= 2delta - 2
148|    |       and l >= p - delta + 2.
149|    |     - Step 2 needs N > 3kappa: holds.
150|    |     - Step 3 needs N > 3kappa - 2 and N > 2p: holds; as kappa - 1 >= kappa0 + 1, it uses the accepted SLZ3 at kappa - 1,
151|    |       whose bound 3(kappa - 1) + 1 = 3kappa - 2 suffices (no strengthened induction hypothesis); Step 1 applied to Q' gives
152|    |       D >= 0.
153|    |     - Step 4 needs |Q| = p + delta >= p + 3 and kappa <= p - 1 (S_Q nonempty): holds.  Steps 5 and 8 are independent of kappa.
154|    |     - Step 6 needs D <= 2delta - 2 and m >= (p + 3delta)/2 >= 2delta >= D + 2 (delta <= p): holds.
155|    |     - Step 7 needs D <= 2delta - 2 <= 2p - 6 (delta <= p - 2) and ends with l <= D, i.e. N <= 3kappa: contradiction.
156|    |     - Step 9 is the only step whose input changes: SLZ3's 'l >= kappa + 2 (base >= 4)' is replaced by l = kappa + 1 and
157|    |       base := (3l - 2p)/2 = (3kappa + 3 - 2p)/2, an integer with base >= 3 iff 3kappa >= 2p + 3 (true in case (iii));
158|    |       base >= 3 is all that S1-sharp's Step 9 with erratum A-m6 uses (t' <= 2 gives X < 0; t' >= 3 uses t' - 2 > t' - base),
159|    |       and l >= p - delta + 2 > b_min (Step 1) completes the contradiction.  (Claude's review records that base >= 4 in fact
160|    |       holds throughout case (iii); not needed.)
161|    |     Hence N <= 3kappa in case (iii).

7|## Finding (confirmed)
8|
9|Pi_need < 0 can occur inside the hypotheses of Theorem SEG: an avoider has A <= 2p - 1 and, after the normalisation A >= B,
10|B <= kappa (K0star-r2 (Z0)), so R = A + B can exceed 2p when B >= 2 (e.g. parameters p = 11, kappa = 8, k = 12, R = 26 satisfy
11|R = 2p - k + 2 kappa, k >= p + 1, A <= 21 forces B >= 5 < kappa).  For such words the printed proof line
12|"sum t_j <= Pi_need" is false (the least admissible t_j are 0), and the printed (H1) m_hi - m_lo >= Pi_need + 1 can be <= 0,
13|whereas the proof needs the base value m_b in {m_lo, m_lo + 1} to satisfy m_b <= m_hi.  So the printed hypotheses do not
14|support the printed proof when Pi_need < 0.  The raw statement is therefore CORRECTED (narrowed, explicitly) as follows.
15|
16|## Correction
17|
18|Put Pi+ := max(0, Pi_need).  In Theorem SEG (v5 Sec. 5) read
19|   (H1') m_hi - m_lo >= Pi+ + 1   and   (H3') g_E >= Pi+ + 1
20|in place of (H1), (H3); in its proof read "sum t_j <= Pi+" and "m in [m_lo, m_lo + 1 + Pi+]".  With these hypotheses the proof
21|is unchanged: when Pi_need <= 0 all t_j = 0, every configuration is the base with m_b in {m_lo, m_lo + 1}, inside [m_lo, m_hi]
22|by (H1'), and Lemma 3.2 applies because the window sum floor(E+/2) + floor(E-/2) >= ceil(R/2) - 1 >= p - 1 - Pi_need >= p - 1
23|already (total gain >= 0 >= Pi_need).  (H3') is then automatic except for g_E = 0, where it is not needed either; it is kept
24|only to make the statement uniform.  For Pi_need >= 0, (H1') = (H1) and (H3') = (H3): nothing changes.
25|
26|## Effect on the corollaries (no change)
27|
28|Every application of Theorem SEG has Pi_need >= 1, so the correction does not touch them:
29|* SEG-p and MID: R = p, Pi_need = (p - 1)/2.
30|* SEG-num: R = p + delta with 5 delta <= p - 10, so R < 2p - 1 and Pi_need >= 1.
31|* SEG-k (separate addendum, not in this review set): if R >= 2p - 1 then k >= p + floor(R/2) + 1 >= 2p, hence
32|  R = 2p - k + 2 kappa <= 2 kappa <= 2p - 2, a contradiction; so R <= 2p - 2 and Pi_need >= 1 there.
33|K2P+, TOP and Lemmas ARC+, SEG, 3.2, BAL do not use the printed (H1)/(H3) of Theorem SEG.  K2P+ has R <= 2 kappa - 1 <= 2p - 3.

102|Statement.  Let w be in setting (S) with B = 0 and R <= 2 kappa + 1, so m* := kappa + 1 - floor(R/2) >= 1.  Choose the cyclic
103|start so that the run R_1 is nonempty if R is odd (any start if R is even); index the blocks S_1, ..., S_k from there, put
104|rho_j := r_1 + ... + r_j, eta_j := y_j/2 + rho_j in Z_p, and for a set Z of blocks eta(Z) := {eta_j : j in Z} (a set of
105|residues).  If w avoids, then for every partition of {1, ..., k} into 2m* nonempty consecutive segments Z_1 < ... < Z_(2m*)
106|(linear order), sum_i |eta(Z_i)| <= p + 2m* - 2.  (Such partitions exist: k - 2m* = 2p - 2 - [R odd] >= 0.)
107|Proof details.  For a_i in Z_(2i-1), b_i in Z_(2i), P := union over i of the runs R_(a_i + 1), ..., R_(b_i): the intervals are
108|nonempty, consecutive ones are separated by R_(b_i + 1), ..., R_(a_(i+1)), and the wrap-around gap contains R_1, so
109|m(P) = m* and P is proper.  With B = 0, x := E+(P) = D_P = sum_i (rho_(b_i) - rho_(a_i)), and u_P - D_P =
110|sum_i (eta_(a_i) - eta_(b_i)).  "G_(m*)" in v5 means gamma_(m*), evaluated directly (not by Fact 2.1):
111|gamma_(m*)(E) = min(H + floor(R/2), E - floor(R/2), floor(E/2)), as E - kappa + m* - 1 = E - floor(R/2) and
112|p - m* = H + floor(R/2).  The window I(P) = [-gamma_(m*)(R - x), gamma_(m*)(x)] is:
113|  R even, 0 <= x <= R: {x - R/2};   R odd, 1 <= x <= R - 1: {x - ceil(R/2), x - floor(R/2)};
114|  R odd, x = 0: {-floor(R/2)};      R odd, x = R: {floor(R/2)} (which misses x - floor(R/2)).
115|If R is odd, R_1 is nonempty and not in P, so x <= R - 1.  Hence x - floor(R/2) lies in I(P) in every case, and avoidance
116|(Lemma W) forbids sum_i (eta_(a_i) - eta_(b_i)) = -floor(R/2) (mod p).  These sums form the sumset
117|eta(Z_1) - eta(Z_2) + ... - eta(Z_(2m*)), which by (CD) has >= min(p, sum_i |eta(Z_i)| - 2m* + 1) residues; it misses one, so
118|sum_i |eta(Z_i)| - 2m* + 1 <= p - 1.  []

121|gain >= Pi_need: sum t_j <= Pi_need" by the following.  An optional pair is two single-block arcs and raises m by one; a base
122|pair-arc is one arc.  The certified gain of a configuration family is G_b + sum_j g_j(t_j), G_b := number of base pair-arcs,
123|g_j(t) := min(p - 1, 2t, b'_j - 1) (I.3).
124|* Pi_need >= 1.  By Fact 2.2 (R >= p), Pi_need <= (p - 1)/2.  Start with all t_j = 0; while the certified gain is < Pi_need,
125|  raise by one some t_j with 2t_j < b'_j - 1.  Such a j exists: if every t_j is saturated, either some g_j = p - 1 >= Pi_need, or
126|  sum_j g_j equals the uncapped capacity and G_b + sum_j g_j = g_E - |L n O| >= Pi_need by (H3).  Each step raises the certified
127|  gain by >= 1: before it, g_j(t_j) <= (certified gain) < Pi_need <= (p - 1)/2, so g_j(t_j) = 2t_j, and after it
128|  g_j >= min(p - 1, 2t_j + 1) = 2t_j + 1.  Hence the loop stops after at most Pi_need - G_b <= Pi_need steps: sum_j t_j <= Pi_need.

165|## Part II. NEW SINGLE-FAMILY: Theorem SEG for Pi_need < 0 under its printed hypotheses (NOT accepted)
166|
167|Status: new proof branch, first written by the Codex corollary reviewer (7cf9cc10...0880, Sec. 3, lines 83-147), re-derived
168|here step by step; the parent reported checking its algebra.  It is reviewed by one model family only and is NOT accepted.
169|It supersedes erratum 4's narrowing only after a second-family review and a parent verdict.  No application uses it.
170|**Claim.**  Theorem SEG holds as printed when Pi_need < 0 (i.e. R >= 2p + 1): an avoider in setting (S) with (H1)
171|m_hi - m_lo >= Pi_need + 1 and (H2) n_CA >= 2 m_lo + 2 does not exist.  (H3) is not used.
172|Proof.  Put l := m_lo, c := ceil(R/2) >= p + 1, a := ceil((R - d)/4), b := floor((R + d)/4); so m_hi = p - b and
173|Pi_need = p - c.  (H1) reads c - b >= l + 1.  Since a >= (R - d)/4 and b >= (R + d)/4 - 3/4, the integer a + b is
174|>= R/2 - 3/4, hence a + b >= c - 1; so a >= c - 1 - b >= l.  If l >= 2, then l = kappa + 1 - a <= kappa + 1 - l, so
175|2l <= kappa + 1 <= p and l <= (p - 1)/2 (p odd); if l = 1 this bound is trivial.
176|Build and balance the base exactly as in the printed proof (O_0 inside C u AB of size N_O = 2l + 2 by (H2); Lemma BAL; final O
177|with |O| in {N_O - 2, N_O}), and add no segments.  The base has m in {l, l + 1}, so 1 <= m <= (p + 1)/2, and |T| <= d.
178|Put f+- := floor(E+-/2).  Then f+ + f- >= c - 1 >= p (Lemma 3.2's computation) and max(f+, f-) <= b (E_max <= (R + d)/2), so
179|min(f+, f-) >= c - 1 - b >= l >= m - 1.  The lower condition of Fact 2.1 holds: ceil(E_min/2) >= a >= kappa + 1 - l >=
180|kappa + 1 - m.  Hence E - kappa + m - 1 >= floor(E/2) for E = E+-, and gamma_m(E+-) = min(p - m, f+-).  Their sum is >= p - 1:
181|with no clipping it is >= p; with one clipped term it is >= (p - m) + (m - 1) = p - 1; with both clipped it is
182|2(p - m) >= p - 1 as m <= (p + 1)/2.  So the common window of the base patterns has >= p integers and contains every residue;
183|any base pattern (Lemma 2.1) then gives an identity 2p-subword by Lemma W, a contradiction.  []
184|Shape remark (Codex): the hypotheses (H1)-(H3) admit Pi_need < 0 together with m_lo > m_hi, e.g. the shape parameters p = 11,
185|R = 25, kappa = 10, d = 3, q = 13 with nine C-runs and four AB-runs (m_lo = 5, m_hi = 4, Pi_need = -2); this is a shape
186|observation only, no avoidance claim.  In that regime the printed floor-window argument is unavailable, which is why the
187|branch uses the clipped endpoints gamma_m(E+-) = min(p - m, f+-).
188|
189|## Scope and what is not covered
190|
191|Ambient conditions of every claim (foundation review, Sec. C): p >= 5 prime; cyclic w of length 4p - 2 over D_(2p); rotation
192|letters in one pair {rho_x, rho_-x}, x != 0, normalised to x = 1 and A >= B; every maximal cyclic constant reflection block odd;
193|F, R >= 1; maximal decomposition (empty runs genuine); no purity, no B < kappa, no restriction on values.  Per-claim scopes
194|are those of the foundation review Sec. C, with I.10 and I.11.
195|Not part of this overlay (separate files, not in any current review set): proof-REFLHI-arcs-v5-addendum-SEGk-v2.md,

7|## D1: Lemma AP' (statement unchanged), corrected proof
8|Let n >= 1, 3n + 2 < p, d := |x~| in [1, (p - 1)/2], I an arc of l <= 2n + 1 consecutive residues containing the n + 1 points
9|a + rho x (0 <= rho <= n).  The n distinct points with 1 <= rho <= n lie in I n (I + x), whose size is
10|max(0, l - d) + max(0, l - p + d).
11|(i) d >= l: l - d <= 0 and l - p + d <= 2d - p < 0, so the intersection is EMPTY: contradiction.
12|(ii) p - l < d < l: l - d < 2l - p and l - p + d < 2l - p, and if both are positive their sum is 2l - p; as 2l - p <= 4n + 2 - p < n,
13|the intersection has fewer than n points: contradiction.
14|(iii) Otherwise d <= p - l and d < l, so l - p + d <= 0 and n <= l - d: d <= l - n <= n + 1.
15|Lifting (induction): L = [alpha, alpha + l - 1] maps onto I, y_0 in L lifts a, y_rho := y_0 + rho x~.  If y_rho is in L then
16|y_(rho+1) is in [alpha - n - 1, alpha + 3n + 1] and its residue is in I, so y_(rho+1) is in L + mp; m >= 1 is impossible as
17|p > 3n + 1, and m <= -1 is impossible as l + n < p.  So y_n, y_0 are in L, n d <= l - 1 <= 2n, d <= 2: x in {1, -1, 2, -2}.  []

19|proof-LS-caseII-v2-addendum-1.md uses Steps 1-4 of proof-LS-caseII-v2.md, whose Step 1 cites Lemma AP: the corrected proof of
20|Lemma AP in erratum-2-prereq-consolidated.md R6 governs there.  proof-STAB-base-p1mod3-v1.md and proof-LSplus-e2-v1.md use neither
21|AP nor AP'.

### SHARD SOURCE 26
Path:/Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/proof-theoremP.md
WholeSHA256:58a85ebeba8208e2f89b39a691235d2d1374656d8888ea905fe96aa75e1dafc2
Bytes:5964;CompleteOriginalLF1-109
1|# Theorem P: a Chevalley–Warning proof of ordered EGZ for a class of dihedral words
2|
3|Status: written proof (lane MathOlson, 2026-10-16), mechanism checked by brute
4|force (`code/verify_marked.py`, `code/verify_theoremP.py`); NOT yet independently
5|reviewed.  This is a theorem about a CLASS OF WORDS in D_{2p}; it does not
6|prove ordered Olson for D_{2p} (the "many odd gaps" class remains open here).
7|
8|## Setting and conventions
9|
10|p is an odd prime. D_{2p} = {(v, e) : v in Z_p, e in {+1,-1}} with
11|(v, e)(v', e') = (v + e v', e e')  (rotations (x,+1), reflections (y,-1)).
12|For a word w = w_1 ... w_N and indices i_1 < ... < i_k the ordered product is
13|w_{i_1} w_{i_2} ... w_{i_k}.  An identity k-subword is such an index set with
14|product (0, +1).  Cyclic rotation of w preserves the existence of identity
15|k-subwords (a product U V becomes V U = U^{-1} (U V) U).
16|
17|Let w contain F >= 1 reflections.  Rotate w cyclically so that it ENDS with a
18|reflection:
19|    w = X_1 f_1 X_2 f_2 ... X_F f_F,
20|where f_t = (y_t, -1) are the reflections and X_t (t = 1..F) is the block of
21|rotations strictly between f_{t-1} and f_t (X_1 = rotations before f_1); n_t =
22||X_t|.  ("Gap t" = X_t; cyclically these are exactly the gaps between
23|consecutive reflections.)
24|
25|## Theorem P
26|
27|If N = |w| <= 4p - 1 and  F + sum_{t=1}^{F} floor(n_t / 2) >= 3p - 1,  then w
28|has an identity 2p-subword.
29|
30|## Proof
31|
32|**Step 1 (parametrisation).** In each gap X_t list the rotations in index order
33|and group them into floor(n_t/2) consecutive PAIRS pi = (i, i'); if n_t is odd
34|the last rotation of X_t is a SINGLETON.  Introduce Boolean parameters
35|  u_2, ..., u_F  (gap colours; set u_1 := 0 and u_{F+1} := u_1 = 0),
36|  m_pi  for each pair pi  (pair marks).
37|Number of parameters:  M = (F - 1) + sum_t floor(n_t/2) >= 3p - 2.
38|
39|To a parameter point (u, m) assign the selection S(u, m):
40|* reflection f_t is selected iff u_t != u_{t+1}  (t = 1..F);
41|* both rotations of pair pi in gap t are selected iff m_pi XOR u_t = 1,
42|  otherwise neither;
43|* singletons are never selected.
44|
45|**Step 2 (injectivity; the empty selection).** The selection determines the
46|parameters: going left to right, u_1 = 0 and u_{t+1} = u_t XOR [f_t selected]
47|determine all u_t; then m_pi = [pi selected] XOR u_t.  Hence the map
48|(u, m) -> S(u, m) is injective, and S(u, m) is EMPTY iff (u, m) = 0: an empty
49|selection selects no reflection, so all u_t = u_1 = 0, and no pair, so all
50|m_pi = u_t = 0.
51|
52|**Step 3 (size).** |S(u,m)| = #{t : u_t != u_{t+1}} + 2 #{pi : m_pi XOR u_{t(pi)} = 1}.
53|The first term is the number of colour changes around the cycle 1..F, 1, hence
54|even.  So |S| is always EVEN, and |S| <= N <= 4p - 1.
55|
56|**Step 4 (signs and the product).** Let S = S(u,m).  For an element of S lying
57|in gap t or equal to f_t, the number of selected reflections strictly before
58|it is #{s < t : u_s != u_{s+1}}, which has the parity of [u_t != u_1] = u_t.
59|So in the ordered product every selected rotation x in gap t is multiplied by
60|c_t := (-1)^{u_t}, and a selected reflection f_t contributes c_t y_t to the
61|rotation coordinate.  The number of selected reflections is even (Step 3), so
62|the product is (V, +1) with
63|  V = sum_{t : u_t != u_{t+1}} c_t y_t + sum_{pairs pi selected} c_{t(pi)} x_pi,
64|where x_pi = x_i + x_i'.  Using (c_t - c_{t+1})/2 = u_{t+1} - u_t (which is
65|0 when u_t = u_{t+1} and c_t when they differ) and summation by parts with
66|u_1 = u_{F+1} = 0,
67|  sum_{t=1}^{F} (u_{t+1} - u_t) y_t = sum_{t=2}^{F} u_t (y_{t-1} - y_t).
68|For pairs: a selected pair (m XOR u_t = 1) contributes c_t x_pi; one checks
69|c_t x_pi [m XOR u_t] = x_pi m - x_pi u_t for both u_t = 0, 1.  Therefore, in Z_p,
70|  V(u, m) = sum_{t=2}^{F} beta_t u_t + sum_pi x_pi m_pi,
71|  beta_t = y_{t-1} - y_t - sum_{pi in gap t} x_pi.
72|V is a polynomial of degree 1 in the parameters (over F_p), and S(u,m) is an
73|identity subword iff V(u, m) = 0 in Z_p.
74|
75|**Step 5 (Chevalley–Warning on the Boolean box).** Write the size as the
76|integer polynomial
77|  Z(u, m) = sum_{t=1}^{F} (u_t + u_{t+1} - 2 u_t u_{t+1}) + 2 sum_pi (m_pi + u_{t(pi)} - 2 m_pi u_{t(pi)}),
78|which agrees with |S(u, m)| on {0,1}^M and has degree 2.  Over F_p put
79|  Q = (1 - V^{p-1}) (1 - Z^{p-1}),   deg Q <= 3(p - 1) < M.
80|On {0,1}^M, Q(x) = 1 if V(x) = 0 and Z(x) = 0 in F_p, and Q(x) = 0 otherwise
81|(Fermat).  Box lemma: for any polynomial Q over F_p of degree < M,
82|  sum_{x in {0,1}^M} (-1)^{|x|} Q(x) = 0.
83|(Proof: suffices for monomials; a monomial of degree < M omits some variable
84|x_j, and the involution flipping coordinate j pairs points with opposite signs
85|and equal values.)  Hence
86|  sum_{x : V(x) = 0, Z(x) = 0 mod p} (-1)^{|x|} = 0 in F_p.
87|The point x = 0 contributes +1, so there is a parameter point x != 0 with
88|V(x) = 0 and |S(x)| = Z(x) = 0 mod p.
89|
90|**Step 6 (conclusion).** By Step 2, S(x) is nonempty; by Step 3 its size is
91|even and at most 4p - 1; being divisible by p (odd), the size is a positive
92|even multiple of p below 4p, i.e. exactly 2p.  By Step 4 its ordered product is
93|the identity.  QED.
94|
95|## Remarks
96|
97|1. Corollary (reflection coset): any word of 3p-1 reflections in D_{2p} has an
98|   identity 2p-subword (F = 3p-1, all n_t = 0).  Finite-exact computations
99|   (m = 3..9) show 3m-1 is also the sharp threshold for composite m.
100|2. Which words are covered depends only on the R/F type pattern.  Uncovered:
101|   F - #(odd gaps) <= 2p - 2; this contains every extremal (length 4p-2)
102|   dihedral avoider found in the exhaustive D10 data (alternating odd blocks).
103|3. The 2-part (size parity) is handled ONLY through pairing inside gaps.  A
104|   general "mixed modulus" Chevalley–Warning statement that would also control
105|   parity is false (it would give D(G) = D*(G) for G = C_2^4 + C_6).
106|4. Prime powers m = p^k: the same argument with congruences mod p^k would need
107|   Brink's restricted-variable Chevalley theorem for prime-power moduli
108|   (Combinatorica 31 (2011) 127-130); its exact hypotheses have NOT been read
109|   from the source here, so the extension is CONDITIONAL and kept separate.

1|# D1-END — literal self-containment expansion
2|
3|Recorded 2026-10-21T08:15:08-0700 (`/bin/date`). Read after erratumv1. These are the elementary one-line arguments already described in the original proof and both reviews, written literally so a reader need not reconstruct an instruction to add them. No new theorem or scope.
4|
5|Assume w avoids. Define e(x)=(p+1)x in Z/(2p). It is well-defined and additive because p(p+1) is divisible by2p; its values are even and congruent toxmodp, hence it is the unique even lift. E(P)=A_P+B_(P^c)=B+D_P. Therefore psi(P)=e(2u_P)−E(P).
6|
7|If u_P has an integer representative in[-floor((R−E)/2),floor(E/2)], doubling gives an even t in[-(R−E),E] with t congruent toe(2u_P)mod2p. Conversely such an even t has t/2 congruent tou_Pmodp and lies in that floor window. Equivalently psi has a representative q in[-R,0]: setq=t−E in one direction; in the other q+E is EVEN because its residue ise(2u_P), and lies in[-(R−E),E]. The complementary representatives are[1,2p−R−1].
8|
9|For increment h_i congruent toe(theta_i)−d_i mod2p, parity is h_i congruent tod_i congruent tor_i mod2. The increment interval has fewer than2pintegers and contains0, so h_i=0 iff the class vanishes; equivalently d_i is even and theta_i congruent tod_imodp. A genuine empty run has d_i0 and theta_i nonzero, so its increment is even nonzero. P0's avoider hypothesis is now explicit.
10|
11|CaseB: stretch indices of one parity are nonadjacent on the2m0-cycle since their differences are nonzeroeven while adjacent differences are±1. Flipping a proper subset of that parity changes no boundary inside a stretch, removes exactly its two incident old boundaries, and creates none. CaseC: neighbours withinJ share the initial sign and lie in the same proper stretch, so independence on those interior paths is independence on the originalcycle; old-boundary endpoints are never flipped. These justify the already-reviewed sign counts including n=2f.
12|
13|The numerical ledger's universe is[2,p−1] with the stated residual convention; delta2's highband atp11 is empty. No additional prime/parameter/generalization or runtime claim.

1|# Theorem P import closure — parent erratum v2
2|
3|Recorded 2026-10-21T04:22:45-0700 (`/bin/date`). Read after erratumv1. Disposes Claude full-proof review /Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/institute/runs/six-lane-2026-10-18-96h/review/olson-TheoremP-import-closure-claude-v1.prompt.note.md (ad3e8c9b8d52d6445089e1a7163a4ac751ad4fd7279bb1e85e3cf4c72ba9315d):0F/0M/3m, exposition only.
4|
5|1. **Original order:** call the original word AB and the rotated word w′=BA. Cutting after a reflection only cyclically permutes the gap lengths, preserving F,N and sum floor(n_t/2). If P_A,P_B are the selected ordered products in the two parts, the rotated product is P_B P_A and the original product is P_A P_B=P_B^−1(P_B P_A)P_B. Thus the same selected2p positions give identity in the original increasing-index order, including pairs crossing the original boundary.
6|2. **Product formula:** induction from (A,E)(a,e)=(A+Ea,Ee) gives (a_1,e_1)…(a_k,e_k)=(sum_j (product_(l<j)e_l)a_j, product_j e_j). Prefix reflection parity therefore supplies the sign c_t stated in Step4.
7|3. **Notation/degrees:** use degV≤1, degZ≤2 (only upper bounds are needed); write wt(x) for the parameter-vector Hamming weight, distinct from selected length |S(x)|. Write |S(x)|=Z(x)≡0(modp) rather than an equality-to-congruence chain. Codex's exact-degree1 finding is already disposed byv1; weakening the degree2 wording changes no step.
8|
9|The theorem statement and six-step argument are unchanged. No composite/prime-power/sharpness or full ordered Olson conclusion is added. No computation, source mutation or historical reclassification.

23|## Parent derivation
24|
25|Assume an unbalanced longest Z, so S(N)=n_++2pj with j≥1 because 0≤n_+≤2t<2p. Every integer weight in [0,W+n_+] is realized by a submultiset of P=(units of Z)∪Y′ (REALIZE's proof interval plus units). Prefixes N_0 of N with S(N_0)≤m+n_+ give actual zero-sums with available units, so f(N_0)=S(N_0)+|N_0|≤2t. Prefixes with 2p−W≤S(N_0)≤2p+n_+ give the occurrence-disjoint zero-sum (N−N_0)∪X with X⊆P of weight n_++2p−S(N_0); since |X|≥weight−e′, f(N_0)≥2p−e′. The first prefix crossing m+n_+ increases by s≤slack and lands in the second window because W+m≥2L≥2p+2. Combining the two bounds gives L≤2t, contradicting L≥2t+1. ROOM's W-bound uses only longestness; balance gives E=e′+n_+−n_−≤k−1. In ROT-ZB+, DAV and 2kappa^+k≤2p−1 give kappa^≤(2p−2)/3, so S1-sharp supplies (S1); SHgen(H) is 2p−2−2kappa^≥k−1≥E and SHgen(K) uses KETA's k-free inequality with k≤p−1. The corollary's F≥2o_F−1 is equivalent to 2kappa(w)+o_F≤2p−1 and transfers to every admissible normal form; deletion cases preserve both conditions.

36|## External theorem and parent primary re-read
37|
38|Parent re-read [arXiv:2110.01081v3](https://arxiv.org/html/2110.01081v3) (HTML, dated 01 Jun 2022) at this verdict. Theorem 2: "Suppose that α=(a1,…,an) is a zero-sum-free sequence of elements of an abelian group. If |Σ(α)|<2n then …" there are a and integers 1=x1<=…<=xn with x(k+1)<=x1+…+xk, a_k=x_k a, Σ(α)={0,a,…,(x1+…+xn)a}, and x1+…+xn<ord(a) for finite order. Σ(α) includes the empty subsequence. **No length hypothesis appears**; the paper states the conclusion holds when the sum set is small "not necessarily because 'the whole group is exhausted'". This closes Claude's load-bearing check: at (p,kappa,delta')=(19,13,1) the core has n=9<p/2, and a half-order hypothesis (Theorem 1, Savchev-Chen/Yuan) would not have covered it. The proof consumes only the hypothesis and the structure/interval conclusion; a≠0, ord a=p, n<=S and the occurrence-level coefficient interval are derived in the proof. Lev's proof is not replayed, and the local transcription hash is not a remote byte hash.

1|# Ordered Olson: sharp odd-sequence lemma and uniform restricted dihedral consequence
2|
3|Recorded at 2026-10-19T00:33:24-0700.
4|
5|**CORRECTNESS-ACCEPTED SCOPED PARTIAL, relative to declared classical imports.** Lemma S1-sharp holds for every prime p≥5 and integer 0≤κ≤floor(2p/3). Consequently the accepted dihedral INV*/ordered-Olson route now covers **every prime p≥5** under **3o_F≤2p+1**, with no extra side condition on F. The previous terminal gap (p,k,κhat)=(31,21,20) is closed. This does not prove unrestricted INV*, ordered Olson for all D_2p, or ordered Olson for all finite groups.
6|
7|## Exact statements
8|
9|1. For any sequence Y of odd elements of Z_(2p), if Y has no zero-sum subsequence of length in [2κ+2,2p], then every zero-sum subsequence has length≤2κ, provided0≤κ≤floor(2p/3). No bound on the length of Y is assumed. The threshold is sharp: the implication fails at every κ in [floor(2p/3)+1,p] by the explicit F1 family; κ≥p+1 is not a failure claim.
10|2. Every length-(4p−2) avoider over D_2p with3o_F≤2p+1 satisfies INV* (rotations in one nonzero pair and constant maximal reflection runs). Here o_F counts odd maximal cyclic constant reflection blocks, not arbitrary reflection runs.
11|3. Every length-(4p−1) word over D_2p with3o_F≤2p+1 has an identity2p-subword in increasing index order.

23|Induct onκ, using the accepted length-free S1+ forκ≤(p+1)/2. Setu=2κ=p+δ. A shortest long zero-sumT has evenN≥2p+2; every proper nonempty zero-sum lies in[l,u], l=N−u, and is minimal because a nested split would forceN≤3κ≤2p. Applying the induction toT itself forces a properQ of lengthu; Q′ has lengthl andD=u−l≥0.

25|The imported index-one theorem applies toQ over the composite cyclic groupC_(2p), since|Q|≥p+3. Its basis element has order2p: orders1/p give even elements, order2 would force length2. ThusQ has positive odd coefficients summing2p. WritingQ=g^m S gives mass(S)=p−δ, m≥(p+3δ)/2 andb_min≤p−δ+1.
26|
27|For occurrence-submultisetsQ1⊆Q,W⊆Q′ with equal coefficient sum modulo2p, excluding exactly the empty/full exchange cases, (Q−Q1)W is a proper zero-sum; its length gives |W|≤|Q1|≤|W|+D. The Step6 inclusion-minimalZ0 construction produces aQ1 with too many terms for anyQ′coefficient≥D+2: the |Z0|=1 and |Z0|≥2 inequalities separately contradict the mass bounds. IfQ′coefficient sum≥4p, a first crossing of2p and its predecessor give mass(W)≥2p and≤2p−l+D, forcingp≤3δ−4, impossible. HenceQ′also has coefficient sum2p.

29|The same exchange forbids representingb_min as two or moreQ′terms. Thus t′<b_min and its low nonunit terms have total≤b_min−t′−1. The exact identity Σ_Y(c−3)/2=t′−base, with integralbase=(3l−2p)/2≥3, excludes t′≤2 and permits at most oneYterm above t′. Therefore l≤b_min, contradicting l≥p−δ+2>b_min. This closes the induction without the previous S1(g) Steps3–8 or the exploratory search.
